# Panel script

Draw-ready script for every chapter of *Harry Potter and the Philosopher’s Stone* in this book. Twenty-one chapters. First Lessons is its own chapter. Nicolas Flamel stays before the Mirror of Erised.

Image generation uses the picture brief: what is physically happening, what each person feels, and what the face and body show. The balloon underneath is restricted speech. Do not pass the balloon to the image model, and do not treat a heard line as the drawing instruction.

Image generation has not been started. Panels with no file stay empty frames. The flipbook shows the caption and the dialogue in that frame. Existing files stay on the panel they already belong to.

A scene is one place and one stretch of time, and it takes at least one page. A new place opens with the outside, then the threshold, then what people were already doing, then talk. Magic is a sequence: who aims, the thin link, the result, and a follow-through when a body changes.

Counts: 21 chapters, 210 pages, 1219 panels.

## Chapter 1 — The Boy Who Lived

### Page 1 — The street before midnight

**Panel 1.** Privet Drive from the corner. Every lamp is still lit. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

A wet suburban street seen from the corner, identical brick houses stepping away into fog. Street lamps burn warm yellow. Pavement shines. No one is walking. The street is empty. Even the windows look asleep.

**Panel 2.** Number four, from the gate. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

The nearest house, number four, square and proud of its hedge. A milk bottle waits on the step. One window is warm. The door is shut and ordinary. A closed house. Nothing welcomes anyone.

**Panel 3.** Through the glass, the room, not the people yet. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

From the garden side of the window: a floral sofa, a television, too many baby photographs, a lamp with a fringed shade. The room is whole and fussy. The sleepers are only shapes in it. The room looks pleased with itself.

**Panel 4.** They were asleep before anyone arrived. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

Closer through the glass, still outside. Vernon is slumped in a brown suit. Petunia is asleep in a mint housedress. Baby Dudley sleeps in a frilly crib. None of them faces the window. All three are slack, mouths open, dead to the street.

**Panel 5.** A tabby has been on the wall for hours. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

A thin brown tabby with spectacle markings sits on the low garden wall, no collar, tail tucked. She watches the lamps. A moth bats the globe and she does not blink. She has been there all day. The cat is perfectly still, ears forward, disgusted by the window and patient with the street.

**Panel 6.** Someone tall steps into the far end of the street. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

At the dark end of Privet Drive, a very tall thin figure in a plum cloak and a pointed hat comes into the lamplight. A silver lighter-shaped device is closed in his hand. He has not raised it. The cat sees him first. Dumbledore looks along the lamps, calm, not yet at the cat. The cat's eyes lock on him.


### Page 2 — The lamps go out

**Panel 7.** Privet Drive, a few minutes before midnight. Art file: `art/c1-p1-p1.jpg`.

Privet Drive just before midnight. Wet pavement, identical brick houses, fog at knee height. A thin tabby sits on a garden wall. The cat stares down the street, ears forward, perfectly still.

**Panel 8.** He aims the silver lighter. No ribbon yet. Art file: `art/c1-p1-p2.jpg`.

Dumbledore stands at the left edge of Privet Drive, plum cloak and pointed hat. The silver Deluminator, a cigarette-lighter, not a wand, is raised toward the nearest street lamp. Nothing has left it. That lamp is still fully lit. Lamps farther down the street are still lit. A thin brown tabby watches from the wall. His wand stays in his sleeve. Dumbledore is intent on the lamp. The cat is patient.

**Panel 801.** A thin ribbon runs from the lamp into the lighter. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

The same lamp, a moment later. A thin ribbon of warm yellow runs from the globe into the silver Deluminator. The ribbon is a thread, not a flood. The lamp is dimming. His wand is not out. The tabby is small on the wall. Dumbledore watches the ribbon. The cat does not blink.

**Panel 802.** The lamp is out. The street dies in one direction. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

That lamp is dark. Lamps behind him are already out. Lamps farther ahead are still lit, so the street dies in one direction. The Deluminator is lowered. No ribbon remains. The tabby's eyes hold the last of the yellow. The street is quieter. The cat is a witness.

**Panel 9.** The cat watches the last lamp go out. Art file: `art/c1-p1-p3.jpg`.

Tight on the tabby’s face. The last lamp’s light is leaving the globe, a bright coin in each eye, then half gone. Wet brick and a few pale flowers. Eyes wide and knowing. The cat stares toward where Dumbledore stands, out of frame.

**Panel 10.** Dumbledore turns and notices the cat. Art file: `art/c1-p1-p4.jpg`.

Dumbledore has turned toward a low garden wall. A thin brown tabby with no collar sits there, still a cat. The street lamps behind them are already out. He looks at the cat, recognizing her. The cat looks back, ears forward, perfectly still.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Dumbledore:** I should have known that you would be here, Professor McGonagall.

**McGonagall:** Good evening, Professor Dumbledore.


### Page 3 — The cat becomes a woman

**Panel 803.** The same eyes, halfway from cat to woman. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

On the garden wall, the tabby is halfway to a woman. The same eyes. The spectacle markings are becoming square spectacles. One foreleg is an arm, emerald cloth gathering, the other still a paw. No wand. No second cat. Dumbledore has not moved. The changing face is McGonagall's, stern already. Dumbledore waits.

**Panel 11.** The cat becomes Professor McGonagall. Art file: `art/c1-p1-p5.jpg`.

The wall is empty. McGonagall stands on the pavement where the cat was, emerald robes still settling, a wisp of tabby mist at her hem. She looks up, stern and worried, mouth already open to argue.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**McGonagall:** Professor Dumbledore. I have been a cat on that wall since morning.

**Panel 804.** They walk to the door of number four. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

They have left the wall. Plum cloak and emerald robes, the hem still settling, cross a few paces of wet pavement to the dark door. A milk bottle sits on the step. They have not started the step conversation yet. Both are grave. The house is ordinary.

**Panel 12.** McGonagall and Dumbledore on the doorstep of number four. Art file: `art/c1-p1-p6.jpg`.

McGonagall and Dumbledore on the doorstep of number four. The dark front door sits between them. A milk bottle and trimmed hedges. She looks at him, exasperated and afraid. He looks at the door, gentle and already decided.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**McGonagall:** The Dursleys. Muggles of the worst sort.

**Dumbledore:** And yet the boy must be left at number four, Privet Drive.


### Page 4 — A lemon sweet

**Panel 13.** On the step, he finds a paper twist. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

Dumbledore and McGonagall stand at the dark door of number four, where they have already been talking. He reaches into the plum cloak and brings out a small paper twist. The street behind them is already lamp-dark. He looks mildly hopeful. She is still stern, eyes on the door, not on his hand.

**Panel 14.** A lemon sweet, offered. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

A single yellow lemon sweet sits in his long palm. He holds it out to her, between them, under the dead lamp. His brows lift, a private kindness. She looks at the sweet as if it had no business on this night.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Dumbledore:** A lemon sweet, Minerva?

**Panel 15.** She will not take it. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

McGonagall's hands stay in her sleeves. The sweet remains in his palm. Behind her, the warm window of the wrong family. She is offended that the night has room for a sweet. He is not wounded by the refusal.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**McGonagall:** No. Thank you, Professor Dumbledore.

**Panel 16.** He eats it himself. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

He puts the lemon sweet in his own mouth. A small sour wince, almost a smile, and then his face is grave again. The paper twist is empty. The wince is tiny. The sadness was already there. She does not smile.

**Panel 17.** The twist goes back into the cloak. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

He folds the paper away. His other hand is empty. He looks at the door, the decision already made. She looks through the window at the sleepers. He is gentle and finished with the sweet. She is back to disgust.

**Panel 18.** They turn to the glass. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

The two of them are small against the house. The next thing the reader sees is the family inside. No motorcycle yet. The sweet is over. McGonagall's mouth is tight. Dumbledore's eyes are sad, not joking.


### Page 5 — The wrong family

**Panel 19.** Inside, the Dursleys sleep through the night. Art file: `art/c1-p2-p1.jpg`.

Through the front window: Vernon asleep in a brown suit, Petunia asleep in a mint housedress, baby Dudley in a frilly crib. Warm light, a floral sofa, too many baby photos. All three are slack with sleep. None of them look toward the window.

**Panel 20.** McGonagall in profile outside the window, one hand half-raised, the warm room soft behind the glass. Art file: `art/c1-p2-p2.jpg`.

McGonagall in profile outside the window, one hand half-raised, the warm room soft behind the glass. Disgust and pity. She looks at Vernon, then at the blond baby.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**McGonagall:** You cannot mean to leave a wizard child with these people. There is not a scrap of kindness in that sitting room.

**Panel 21.** Dumbledore from below, beard and hat against the black sky, one hand open. Art file: `art/c1-p2-p3.jpg`.

Dumbledore from below, beard and hat against the black sky, one hand open. A few stars show where the cloud has torn. Sad and kind, looking slightly down. He is not smiling.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Dumbledore:** He will be famous, Minerva. Books will be written about him. Fame is a cruel weight for a child who cannot yet speak.

**Panel 22.** Something heavy comes down out of the cloud. Art file: `art/c1-p2-p4.jpg`.

Most of the frame is sky over Privet Drive rooftops, chimneys, and aerials. A bulge hangs in the cloud. Dumbledore and McGonagall are small at the bottom. McGonagall looks alarmed, eyes up. Dumbledore looks up with recognition, almost relief.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**McGonagall:** What in Merlin's name is that?

**Dumbledore:** Hagrid. I asked him to bring the child.

**Panel 23.** A flying motorcycle drops out of the sky. Art file: `art/c1-p2-p5.jpg`.

A huge flying motorcycle drops through the fog above the hedge, headlamp cutting the dark, about to touch down. Hagrid cradles a green bundle. Hagrid looks wrecked, eyes red, staring at the doorstep, not at the two wizards.

**Panel 24.** Hagrid lands with the baby. Art file: `art/c1-p2-p6.jpg`.

The motorcycle has stopped on the drive. Hagrid holds the tiny green bundle. Dumbledore and McGonagall step in from either side. Crushed flowers, long headlamp shadows. Hagrid looks at Dumbledore, begging and grieving. Dumbledore looks at the bundle. McGonagall looks at Hagrid, softened.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Hagrid:** Professor Dumbledore, sir. Professor McGonagall. I've got little Harry.


### Page 6 — The night Voldemort fell

**Panel 25.** Hagrid's voice breaks. Art file: `art/c1-p3-p1.jpg`.

Close on Hagrid’s face and the top of the baby’s black hair. Headlamp glow, an out-of-focus hedge. He looks down at Harry, mouth crumpled, trying to speak. Eyes shining.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Hagrid:** I got him out of the house. I got him out, but James and Lily...

**Panel 27.** Hagrid holds Harry. Art file: `art/c1-p3-p3.jpg`.

Hagrid holds Harry. McGonagall is close, one hand lifted and stopping. Dumbledore stands behind them, hat brim low. The motorcycle ticks as it cools. Hagrid looks wrecked. McGonagall looks at the baby, eyes wet, mouth tight. Dumbledore looks into the dark.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Hagrid:** They're dead. Both of them. James and Lily Potter.

**Panel 28.** Hagrid hunches over the bright green blanket. Art file: `art/c1-p3-p4.jpg`.

Hagrid hunches over the bright green blanket. Dumbledore’s hand enters, palm down, a calming gesture. Fog around their knees. Hagrid flinches, eyes squeezed, looking at the hand. Dumbledore’s visible eye is steady and sad.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Hagrid:** It was You-Know-Who. He killed them. And he tried for the baby an' all.

**Dumbledore:** Say the name, Hagrid. Voldemort failed.

**Panel 29.** The blanket is folded back. The scar is on his right brow. Art file: `art/c1-p3-p5.jpg`.

The baby’s face fills the panel, looking straight out, so his anatomical right brow is on the viewer’s left. A fresh lightning cut runs from that brow up into the black tuft. It is not on the left brow, the chin, or the cheek. A purple sleeve, a huge thumb, and emerald cloth frame the green blanket. Fog and the dark door are only edges. Baby Harry looks straight out, calm, not crying. The scar is the fresh cut on his right brow. Dumbledore looks at that brow. Hagrid looks at the baby’s eyes.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Dumbledore:** See the scar. A lightning cut. That is where the curse struck.

**Panel 30.** Hagrid sobs. Art file: `art/c1-p3-p6.jpg`.

McGonagall in profile, hat against her chest. Hagrid hides his face in one hand. Harry is awake between them. The doorstep enters the bottom of the frame. McGonagall looks at Harry, awe under the sternness. Hagrid’s eyes are shut. Harry looks into the dark.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**McGonagall:** So this is the boy who lived.

**Hagrid:** Aye. And he's only a baby.

**Panel 31.** Hagrid kneels so his face meets Dumbledore’s chest, one hand open toward the house. Art file: `art/c1-p4-p1.jpg`.

Hagrid kneels so his face meets Dumbledore’s chest, one hand open toward the house. Dumbledore holds the baby. McGonagall stands aside, arms folded. A milk bottle, dark windows. Hagrid looks up, pleading. Dumbledore looks down with love and refusal. McGonagall looks at the door, angry that she agrees.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Hagrid:** Don't leave him here, sir. Please. Anywhere but with them.

Inside the nursery, before the curse. Lily, long dark-red hair, pale gown, is on the viewer's left of the crib, already standing guard. **Panel 808.** The wand comes up. No green yet. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

From the nursery doorway, the same unreadable figure raises a wand toward the room. No green has left the tip. Lily stays on the viewer's left. James stays on the viewer's right. The baby is between them. The figure's mouth is hidden. The parents have seen the wand.

**Panel 809.** A thin green spear leaves the wand. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

A thin spear of green light leaves the wand tip and crosses the nursery toward Lily. It has not reached her. It does not fill the room. Lily, viewer's left, raises a hand. James, viewer's right, is already falling out of the line. The baby is behind her. Lily's mouth is open. The green is a thread.

**Panel 26.** The cottage is open. James is down. Lily is by the crib. Art file: `art/c1-p3-p2.jpg`.

The result, still the cottage and not the drive. The roof is blown open. James lies face-down, glasses aside, on the viewer's right of the crib. Lily has collapsed by the crib on the viewer's left, one arm reaching, long dark-red hair, a pale gown. The green spear is gone. The baby is a small shape in the crib. No second room. No living faces to read. The reaching hand is the feeling of the picture.

## Chapter 2 — Harry’s life with the Dursleys

### Page 1 — Morning on Privet Drive

**Panel 1.** Number four in ordinary daylight. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

The same brick house, now morning. A neat lawn, the same hedge, a car in the drive. Nothing looks magical. Neighbors' houses match it down to the curtains. A proud, ordinary house.

**Panel 2.** The front door, from the path. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

The door of number four, brass numbers, a mail slot, a doormat. Petunia's shadow moves behind the frosted glass, busy, not opening the door. The house is already in a rush that does not include a small boy.

**Panel 3.** The hall, before the cupboard opens. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

Inside the hall: floral carpet, a row of Dudley's framed photos climbing the stairs, Vernon's coat on a hook. The cupboard door under the stairs is shut. A bolt is on the outside. The hall is crowded with one child's life and a locked door.

**Panel 4.** Dudley was already counting in the kitchen. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

Through the kitchen doorway, Dudley sits in a striped shirt among a mountain of bright parcels, mouth open, mid-count, delighted with himself. Vernon beams over a newspaper. The table is a feast. Dudley is greedy and happy. Vernon is proud. Neither looks toward the hall.

**Panel 5.** Petunia sets one slice of toast. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

At the end of the table, away from the parcels, one plate holds a single slice of toast. Petunia sets it down without looking at it, then turns back to Dudley's pile. She is brisk and blank. The toast is an afterthought.

**Panel 6.** The cupboard door, still shut. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

Back in the hall, close on the cupboard under the stairs. The bolt. A thin line of dark inside. A small movement, not yet a hand. The kitchen noise is far away. The door looks like a cupboard. It is a bedroom.


### Page 2 — The cupboard

**Panel 7.** Harry wakes in the cupboard under the stairs. Art file: `art/c2-p1-p1.jpg`.

A small hand pushes the cupboard door from inside. Dusty light cuts across cobwebs, a thin mattress, and the sloped underside of the stairs. Sleepy and wary. He looks out into the hall light as if checking whether it is safe.

**Panel 8.** He sits up under the stairs. Art file: `art/c2-p1-p2.jpg`.

Harry sits under the stairs, knees up, the ceiling too low to stand. The open door frames a polished banister and a bright kitchen. He looks at his own knees, resigned. This is an ordinary morning.

**Panel 9.** Dudley counts a mountain of presents. Art file: `art/c2-p1-p3.jpg`.

The sitting room is buried in wrapping paper. Dudley stands among the presents. Harry is a thin figure in the doorway. Dudley looks at the next present, delighted. Vernon and Petunia look at Dudley. Harry looks at the pile, blank.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Dudley:** Thirty-six! Count them, Dad, thirty-six presents!

**Vernon:** And a fine boy deserves every one.

**Panel 10.** Toast for Harry. A feast for Dudley. Art file: `art/c2-p1-p4.jpg`.

The kitchen table is split. One dry slice of toast on the left. Eggs, bacon, and a stack of toast on the right. Harry looks at his toast. Dudley checks that Harry's plate is smaller. Petunia watches Dudley's plate. Vernon hides in the paper.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Petunia:** Eat your toast. And don't you stare at Dudley's plate.

**Panel 11.** Vernon lowers the paper just enough to show his eyes and mustache. Art file: `art/c2-p1-p5.jpg`.

Vernon lowers the paper just enough to show his eyes and mustache. One thick finger points at Harry. The cupboard door is ajar down the hall. Vernon glares, warning. Harry looks back, cautious, mouth closed.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Vernon:** No questions. We do not speak of that rubbish in this house, boy.

**Panel 12.** The family photo has no Harry in it. Art file: `art/c2-p1-p6.jpg`.

A framed photo of Vernon, Petunia, and Dudley hangs on floral wallpaper. Harry stands outside the frame, looking up at the place where he is not. The photo-family looks delighted. Harry looks quiet and hurt.


### Page 3 — The zoo, from the gate

A municipal zoo entrance, balloons, a sign, families queueing. Vernon marches in a brown suit. Petunia holds Dudley's shoulder. The hall of enclosures. Visitors drift. A child taps a lizard tank and is pulled away. At the far end, one large tank is darker than the others. Strangers are casual. Harry is the one who looks properly.

## Chapter 3 — The Keeper of the Keys

### Page 1 — The door

**Panel 1.** The door blasts in. Hagrid fills the frame. Art file: `art/c3-p1-p1.jpg`.

The shack door is off its hinges and falling inward. Hagrid fills the doorway, so large the night sea is only a strip around his shoulders. The bulb swings. Spray blows in. Hagrid's fierceness cracks when he finds Harry. Vernon looks up, mustache shaking. Harry looks up, stunned.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Hagrid:** Sorry about the door.

**Panel 2.** Hagrid hugs Harry off his feet. Art file: `art/c3-p1-p2.jpg`.

Hagrid kneels and hugs Harry off his feet. Only sneakers and one arm show. A squashed cake tin is still in Hagrid's other hand. The Dursleys press against the far wall. Hagrid's eyes are shut, crying and smiling. Harry's visible eye is wide, looking sideways, not sure he is allowed to be held.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Hagrid:** Happy birthday, Harry.

**Panel 3.** Hagrid bends the rifle into a knot. Art file: `art/c3-p1-p3.jpg`.

The rifle barrel is a knot. Vernon's hands are still on the stock. Hagrid uses two fingers. Vernon stares at the knot, belief breaking. Hagrid looks mildly annoyed. Harry watches with a startled almost-smile.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Vernon:** I demand that you leave this instant!

**Hagrid:** Mind the rifle, Mr Dursley. It bends.

**Panel 4.** Hagrid holds out the soggy letter. Art file: `art/c3-p1-p4.jpg`.

A thick damp envelope with a wax seal sits on Hagrid's open palm, bigger than Harry's hand. The cake tin is on the table. The knotted rifle lies on the floor. Hagrid looks encouraging. Harry looks hungry and afraid to hope. Vernon looks at the letter with hate.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Hagrid:** This letter's yours. Hogwarts has been trying to reach you for days.

**Panel 5.** Harry reads the word Hogwarts. Art file: `art/c3-p1-p5.jpg`.

Over Harry's shoulder. The letter is open, a crest at the top. His finger rests under a line. The bulb lights the parchment. Water drips into a pan. Harry's brow is tight with confusion and want. Hagrid watches his face, waiting for the smile.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Harry:** Hogwarts School of Witchcraft and Wizardry.

**Panel 6.** Harry lowers the letter and looks up. Art file: `art/c3-p1-p6.jpg`.

Harry lowers the letter and looks up. He holds it against his chest. Hagrid is a warm blur behind him. Harry looks lost. His eyes are wet from the weather and from being seen.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Harry:** A wizard? I don't even know what that means.

Balloon (not the drawing):

### Page 2 — The flight turns

**Panel 7.** One flight pivots at the bottom. They miss their landing. Art file: `art/c7-p1-p1.jpg`.

A tall Hogwarts stairwell in the morning. Three-quarter view from a landing, low enough to see the bottom hinge and the top end mid-swing. One entire flight turns as a single solid object, pivoting at the bottom like a door hinged at the floor. Every step stays fixed to the other steps. Both railings turn with the flight. The upper end has swung away from the landing Harry and Ron wanted, and a gap is opening. Harry and Ron are on that flight, almost losing balance: feet planted, bodies jerked sideways, hands grabbing the railing that turns with them, a book sliding, robes flying. Students on the landings have stopped and stare. A suit of armor watches with a spear. Other flights are still. Harry looks at the departing landing, alarmed, almost falling. Ron looks at Harry, a wild grin that is also panic, off balance. Students on the landings stare at the turning flight. The armor looks unimpressed.

**Ron:** That was our landing!

**Harry:** It moved. The whole staircase moved.

**Panel 8.** The book slides. The landing stares. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

## Chapter 12 — Quidditch

### Page 1 — The stadium

**Panel 1.** The Quidditch stadium from the grounds. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

The pitch, day, four houses already in the stands: red and gold, yellow and black, blue and bronze, green and silver. Banners. The roar is a picture of open mouths, far. Harry is a small scarlet figure at a tunnel, robes too big, walking in, not out. He looks swallowed by the robe and the noise.

**Panel 3.** The room, whole. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

Cramped stone, benches, scarlet robes, a high window, brooms on a rack including a cleaner one that is his Nimbus. The team is half dressed. The older players look tall. Harry looks eleven.

**Panel 4.** Wood was already on one knee with a chalkboard. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

Oliver Wood, brown hair, leather guard on one arm, kneels with a slate of arrows. He has not given Harry the order about the Snitch. The team leans in. Harry is still standing in the door with the robe pooling on his shoes. Wood is intense. The team is listening. Harry is extra.

**Panel 5.** They argue about the wind, quietly. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

A Beater taps a bat into his palm. A Chaser ties a glove. Someone nods at the window. Harry hears the crowd and not the words. His gloves are too new. The team is busy. Harry is alone inside a group.

**Panel 6.** Wood looks up and sees the first year. No art file yet. The frame stays empty and still shows this caption and any dialogue.

Wood's eyes find Harry. The chalk stops. The next old panel is the plan, spoken. Here, the look is the picture. Wood looks like a captain about to trust a child. Harry tries to look taller and fails.


### Page 2 — The plan

**Panel 7.** The changing room. Wood kneels with the plan. Art file: `art/c11-p1-p1.jpg`.

A cramped stone changing room, red cloth and benches, a high window, the crowd already roaring outside. Harry stands in scarlet Quidditch robes that pool at his shoes, gloves too big, a plain fast broom beside him. Oliver Wood kneels and draws the plan on the bench with a wet finger. The older team fills the room: the red-haired twins with bats, and three taller girls. Wood looks up at Harry, intense, trying to pour a whole game into him. Harry looks at the finger-plan, swallowing, brave and new. The twins look at each other, a reassuring joke, and at Harry, friendly.

**Wood:** Chasers mind the Quaffle. Beaters mind the Bludgers. You, Potter, watch the sky for the Snitch and nothing else.

Wood looks at Harry, willing him to be taller, the doubt badly hidden. Harry looks back, he heard the doubt, and nods anyway with a tight brave smile.

### Page 3 — Brooms up

**Panel 9.** Harry walks out. The stadium is a roar. Art file: `art/c11-p1-p3.jpg`.

The tunnel mouth. Dark stone, then a blast of daylight. Harry steps out small in too-long scarlet robes, broom in hand. Wood is ahead of him on the grass. ### Page 2 — Waking

The hospital wing in summer daylight. White curtains, a high window, empty wooden beds, flowers, a jug of water. He looks at his hands, groggy, trying to remember why they hurt. Confused. Alive. His eyes move toward the glasses, wanting them, not reaching yet.

Glasses on, Harry finds Dumbledore in the chair beside the bed. Long silver beard, half-moon spectacles, purple robes, a closed book on his knee. Sun in his hair. Harry looks at him, trusting, a boy checking the world is still there.

He looks back at the Weasleys first, in the next pages.

**Panel 40.** King's Cross. Molly hugs Harry. Art file: `art/c20-p4-p4.jpg`.

The platform, steam, the scarlet train. Molly Weasley, red hair and a homemade cardigan, gathers Harry in with the same arms she uses for Ron. Ron grins with a suitcase. Hermione waves. Ginny peeks. Molly looks at Harry, maternal, decided. Harry leans into the hug, a boy learning what it is for. Ron looks pleased with the world. Ginny looks curious, not afraid.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Molly:** Harry, dear, come here. You're as thin as Ron, and that is saying something. You write to me if those Muggles are foul to you.

**Panel 41.** Vernon waits. Harry does not hurry. Art file: `art/c20-p4-p5.jpg`.

Through the barrier, the ordinary station. Vernon is the same man as the early chapters: large and beefy, very little neck, a big black mustache, small angry eyes, brown suit, arms folded. Not a new uncle. Petunia is thin, long-necked, blonde hair pinned. Dudley is blond, round, and heavier, bored in a striped shirt. Harry walks toward them with a trunk and Hedwig's cage, no hurry. Vernon looks at Harry, a warning already, the same small angry eyes. Harry looks back, unafraid, polite, chin up. He does not look at the floor. That is the change.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Vernon:** Trunk in the boot. And no funny business until September.

**Harry:** Hello, Uncle Vernon.

**Panel 42.** He looks back, touches the scar, and goes. Art file: `art/c20-p4-p6.jpg`.

Harry at the edge of the Muggle station, one hand on the trunk, the owl's cage beside him. He looks back. Ron and Hermione are still waving. At the car, Vernon is the same large man, big black mustache, brown suit. Summer. A beginning disguised as an ending. Harry looks back, a real smile, small and sure. Ron's wave is big and loyal. Hermione's wave says see you soon. Harry's face is still a child's face. He is coming back.

Balloon (not the drawing):

**Ron:** See you, Harry!

**Hermione:** Write!

**Harry:** I will. I'm coming back.
